# Proposed OEIS comments: A262416 to A268906

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A262416

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A262416>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2367](../papers/2001-2500/2367-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 11*a(n-1) + 48*a(n-2) - 528*a(n-3) - 579*a(n-4) + 6369*a(n-5) + 1612*a(n-6) - 17732*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 6*x^2*(19 + x - 184*x^2 + 14*x^3) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 + 2*x)*(1 - 11*x)*(1 - 13*x^2)*(1 - 31*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Dec 31 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 81 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A262450

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A262450>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2796](../papers/2501-3000/2796-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-4) - 2*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - x - x^2 - x^3 - 2*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Dec 31 2018
> a(n) = floor((2^(n+3)+14)/15). This satisfies the empirical recursion and g.f. - _Robert Israel_, Dec 31 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 15 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A262768

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A262768>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9230](../papers/9001-9500/9230-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -2*(n+2)*(49*n-95)*a(n) + 325*(n-1)*(n+1)*a(n-1) + 2*(67*n-38)*(2*n-5)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 07 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A263053

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263053>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3228](../papers/3001-3500/3228-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A263119

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263119>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2797](../papers/2501-3000/2797-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-4) - 2*a(n-5) = A265381(n+2).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(14 + x + x^2 + x^3 - 14*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 01 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 15 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A263814

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A263814>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [176](../papers/0001-0500/0176-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 82 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 82, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

## A264268

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264268>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [271](../papers/0001-0500/0271-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 52 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 52, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264509

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264509>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [219](../papers/0001-0500/0219-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 69 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 69, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264572

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264572>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [260](../papers/0001-0500/0260-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A265922

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A265922>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [7302](../papers/7001-7500/7302-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) +110*a(n-2) +186*a(n-3) -1189*a(n-4) -2127*a(n-5) +4920*a(n-6) +5776*a(n-7) -9216*a(n-8)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 11 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268554

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268554>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [8708](../papers/8501-9000/8708-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n^3*(2*n-1)*a(n) -6*(4*n-1)*(3*n-1)*(3*n-2)*(4*n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 10 2016
> For integer r and positive integer s, define sequences {u(n) : n >= 0} and {v(n) : n >= 0} by setting u(n) = [x^(s*n)] F(x)^(r*n) and v(n) = [x^(s*n)] E(x)^(r*n). We conjecture that both u(n) and v(n) satisfy the above supercongruences. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```
